# The rows of the probe matrix (cp-gnu-matrix.txt.zst) where this cp does on purpose what GNU cp does not:
# id; this cp's exit status, standard error, standard output (\n for a newline) and the entries after it; GNU's
# answer, the same four, as the matrix holds it for the row's user and family; and the reason, tab-separated. A row whose id starts with `root:` holds
# root's answer where root's differs, read from a run as real root, and is used only as root. A kernel family
# next (`clone:`, `link:`, `mknod:`, `rename:`) uses the row only where the kernel answers as gVisor does (the
# test's probes; see cp-gnu-matrix-gvisor.txt.zst), and `reflink:` only where the test's directory clones (see
# cp-gnu-matrix-reflink.txt.zst); where the reflink overlay replaced a row, a row without a family is not used.
# The reasons:
#   dir-self      `cp -R d d` (and `-t d d`, and `cp -R s b` where b is a link to s): GNU makes d/d, copies d's
#                 entries in inode-number order and refuses on reaching d/d, so it copies f into d/d first only
#                 where f's inode number is below the new d/d's: always on tmpfs, in some rows on ext4 and XFS,
#                 varying from run to run; this cp refuses before it writes anything, the same every time.
#   keep-link     `--remove-destination` onto a link cp follows that is its own destination (`l l`, `l ./l`):
#                 GNU removes the link and then cannot read or link to it; this cp keeps it.
#   keep-relative `cp -s --remove-destination a d`: GNU removes d/a before it refuses the relative link; this
#                 cp refuses first and keeps d/a.
#   backup-link   `cp -fb a a` with a symbolic link at a~: GNU writes through the link; this cp replaces it.
#   keep-data     `--reflink=always` onto an existing file (or the file a link at b leads to) where the clone
#                 fails: GNU truncates it before it asks for the clone; this cp keeps its data, also where that
#                 file has other hard links, which it clones into over the old data.
dir-self-t/R	1	cp: cannot copy a directory, 'd', into itself, 'd/d'\n		 d=dir#755 d/f=A#1#644#0	1	cp: cannot copy a directory, 'd', into itself, 'd/d'\n		 d=dir#755 d/d=dir#755 d/d/f=A#1#644#n d/f=A#1#644#0	dir-self
